# Known differences from CPython 3.12

Draft 1, 2026-10-17, for branch `utf8-str` at 6635b8a (batch 5, 2026-10-19: A46, C30-C38); items C1, C5-C11, C13, C14, C16 fixed on branch `specfix-num`. Companion to [`AIL_SPEC.md`](AIL_SPEC.md).
The promise is the same result as CPython 3.12, or a named compile-time refusal. Every entry below
falls into one of three kinds:

- **declared**: a deliberate difference that is part of the language;
- **refusal**: CPython runs the program, and AIL rejects it at compile time by name (never a wrong
  answer). Section B lists only the cases that matter for the promise. The spec lists the rest;
- **bug**: a difference that is neither declared nor refused. Each one must be fixed, or turned into
  a refusal or a declared difference. "silent" means the program finishes and prints something
  else. "loud" means it stops with an error where CPython answers, or raises a different class.

"P" marks a difference reproduced by a probe program against CPython 3.12.3 on 2026-10-17. Probes
used the default build (general-path fallback on) unless "typed" is noted.

## A. Declared differences

| # | Difference | Reason | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | `int` is exact (CPython's arbitrary precision) on the general path and in every function the default build marks big-int capable; it is signed 64-bit on the typed path, where a result that does not fit is a loud stop (`integer overflow` on stderr, stdout flushed, exit 1; no `except` or `finally` runs). A Python-syntax program gets exact ints when it takes the general path (a function the compiler marks as above, a class, or any other construct only the general path builds) and the typed path only for functions whose ints are locally proven (C218/C219, B1), so a Python-syntax program with an unproven int is built exact and no longer stops with `integer overflow`. A program written in the AIL syntax keeps int64 (catchable `OverflowError`, `wrapping_*`). Literals above int64 are built on the general path and in marked functions. | The typed path keeps C `int64_t` so that a hot integer loop costs nothing extra; the general path pays one tag test and a cold call only after an overflow (docs/design/BIGINT.md). | S §1, R, P |
| A2 | `set` and `frozenset` iterate in insertion order (a removed and re-added element moves to the end), and `set.pop()` removes the first element in that order (it was the newest until branch apigaps2), on the typed path and the general path alike; the result of `| & - ^`, their in-place forms and the named methods keeps a's order, then b's new elements (`^`: a's elements not in b, then b's not in a), and has the left operand's type. CPython follows hash order, which for str changes from run to run. `set.pop()` matches CPython for the common small cases, `{1, 2}.pop()` is 1, `{2, 4, 6}.pop()` is 2; it differs where CPython's hash slots reorder the elements (`{7, 8, 9}.pop()` is 8 in CPython, 7 here; ints beyond the table size, str). Draining a set with `pop()` is linear. | Deterministic output, and the same output whichever path builds the program. Owner decision (spec plan Q2, IR_S4 Q5). | R, P, T: sets_general |
| A3 | Recursion depth guard of 50,000 (stderr `recursion depth exceeded`, exit 1). CPython raises `RecursionError` at about 1,000, so depths between the two succeed in AIL and fail in CPython. `sys.setrecursionlimit` is ignored. | Uses the C stack. 50,000 is measured as safely below the 8 MiB stack limit. | S §2, P |
| A4 | An uncaught exception prints `Kind: message` with no traceback. Exit status 1, the same as CPython. (`ail build --debug` adds a `  raised near file:line` line after the message.) | No traceback machinery. Stderr text is outside the promise. | P |
| A5 | `int ** n` with a negative `n` that is not a constant stops with `negative exponent on an integer` (CPython returns a float). A constant negative exponent gives the float. | Integer `**` is exact int arithmetic. `float(x) ** n` gives the float answer. | S §4, P |
| A6 | Field access or a method call through `None` raises `RuntimeError("field access on None")` (CPython: `AttributeError`). | `AttributeError` is not one of the built exception classes. | R |
| A7 | A tuple comparison that reaches a NaN element raises `ValueError` (CPython answers `False`). | Unverified rationale (the reference states it, no design text). | R, P |
| A8 | `re`: `m.group(i)` of a group that did not take part raises `ValueError` (CPython returns `None`). `re.I` with a non-ASCII subject raises `ValueError`. | There is no `None` str. Case folding tables are not built. | R |
| A9 | `functools.reduce` of an empty iterable with no initial value raises `RuntimeError` with CPython's text (CPython: `TypeError`). | `TypeError` is not one of the built exception classes. | R |
| A10 | `json.loads` of a valid document that does not fit the declared type raises `RuntimeError`. | CPython would return another type, and a silent conversion would print differently. | R |
| A11 | `random` ranges wider than 64 bits raise `RuntimeError`. | int64 (A1). | R |
| A12 | The text of an exception the runtime raised is CPython 3.12's for the traps below, and only in a program that reads the text of an exception (`str(e)`, `repr(e)`, `print(e)`, `f"{e}"`, `"%s" % e`, `.format(e)`, `e.args`, `type(e).__name__` on a name bound by `except X as e`): `int("x")` / `float("x")` (`invalid literal for int() with base 10: 'x'`), division by zero in its six spellings (`division by zero`, `integer division or modulo by zero`, `integer modulo by zero`, `float division by zero`, `float floor division by zero`, `float modulo`), `list index out of range`, `list assignment index out of range`, `string index out of range`, `pop from empty list`, `pop index out of range`, a dict, set or `del` KeyError with its key (`str(e)` is the repr of the key), the file errors with errno and file name (`[Errno 2] No such file or directory: 'x'`), `not enough values to unpack (expected 2, got 1)`, `too many values to unpack (expected 2)`, `list.index` (`9 is not in list`), `str.index` (`substring not found`), `max([])`, `min([])`, `EOF when reading a line`. Every other message the runtime builds (the byte codecs, sockets, `UnicodeDecodeError`, `OverflowError`, the `C ... argument out of range` traps) keeps the wording it had, which is often empty; so does `e.message` of any trap in a program that does not read the text. The text of an uncaught exception on stderr is outside the promise (A4): a KeyError prints its key without quotes there. | Owner decided to align the messages with CPython (IR_S4 Q1). The traps carry their wording only when asked for, so the generated C of every other program is unchanged. | P, T: exc_str |
| A13 | Invalid UTF-8 in text input or in `sys.argv` is an explicit run-time error (CPython: `UnicodeDecodeError` or surrogateescape). | Owner decision (IR_S4 Q4). | R; argv unverified |
| A14 | Brace surface only: a flat `list`/`dict`/`set` is copied by `let ys = xs;`, and an int `and`/`or` gives 1/0 (`0 or 5` is `1`). | The brace surface has value semantics until IR stage S6. Not Python syntax, so outside the CPython promise. | R, P |
| A15 | `float` arithmetic is C's IEEE double, which equals CPython's for single operations. Results that CPython computes with a special algorithm follow that algorithm (`sum()` of floats: see C1; `math.fsum` is not built). | none | S §1 |
| A16 | Text files are UTF-8 unless `encoding=` says `ascii` or `latin-1` (also under their aliases); CPython uses the locale encoding. The two differ only on Windows without `PYTHONUTF8=1`; reference runs use UTF-8 mode. Other encodings are refused (B). | Owner decision Q8 (it is Python 3.15's default, PEP 686). | GENERAL_PURPOSE Q8, T: files |
| A17 | A file is closed by `close()`, `with` or at program exit, never when its last reference goes away. Data written to a file that stays open is flushed at exit, also before an uncaught exception; but a program that writes, drops the file object and reads the path again before exit sees what CPython's refcounting would have flushed only if it called `close()`/`with`. | There is no refcount drop for a class instance on the typed path yet (stage G10, with S5). | T: files |
| A18 | `tell()` of a text file returns the byte offset of the next unread character. CPython returns an opaque cookie, equal to that offset except when the decoder holds state (a lone `\r` read with `newline=None`/`''` at the end of a chunk), where it is a number above 2**64 that only `seek()` understands. `seek(cookie)` takes the offset. | The cookie packs decoder state; no program prints it for a meaning. | P, T: files |
| A19 | `io.UnsupportedOperation` (reading a write-only file, a non-zero relative `seek` on text) is an `OSError` on the general path and is caught by `except OSError` and by `except ValueError` on the typed path; CPython's class is both. | The general runtime has single inheritance. | T: files |
| A20 | `next(f)` at the end of a file raises `RuntimeError("StopIteration")` instead of `StopIteration`. A `UnicodeDecodeError` message names the byte position inside the chunk being decoded, which is the file offset only for the first 8 KiB of a `read(n)`/`readline()` sequence (a plain `read()` decodes the whole rest at once, as CPython does). | `StopIteration` is not one of the built exception classes. | T: files |
| A21 | Files opened `a+` / `ab+` and then read and written alternately may reach the disk at different moments than in CPython (its single read/write buffer is modelled by two). Positions after such a mix follow the rules the fuzz found (`ailf_after_write` in `ailfile_core.h`), not CPython's source. | Buffered I/O internals. | P, T: files |
| A22 | General path only: text read from or written to a file may not contain characters above U+FFFF (the stop that stdin text already has), and a binary file is refused (no `bytes` values). | The general runtime keeps 16-bit strings. | C: `filert.py` |
| A24 | `subprocess.run`: a stream that was not captured is `None` in CPython (`r.stdout`, `e.stderr` ...). There is no `None` for `str`/`bytes` here, so the result class of that call has no such attribute and using it is refused at build time (B). Text output is decoded as UTF-8 (or `ascii` / `latin-1` for `encoding=`) with universal newlines, as A16; CPython uses the locale encoding, which differs only on Windows without `PYTHONUTF8=1`. | No `None` for `str`/`bytes` (Q7 of the design); Owner decision Q8. | T: subprocess |
| A25 | A result is one class per call shape (which streams are captured, `str` or `bytes`), so a variable that is assigned the result of two differently shaped `run()` calls is refused on the typed path (`incompatible class types assigned to r`); `print(r)`, `repr(r)`, `str(r)` of a name bound once to `run(...)` give CPython's `CompletedProcess(args=..., ...)`. On the general path a `str` held in a variable and given as the command is the program name (POSIX; a stop with a message for a Windows target), `CompletedProcess` has a `__repr__`; its `stdout`/`stderr` are `str` only (no `bytes` there). `e.cmd` is a new list (CPython shares the list the call was given). `e.stdout` and `e.stderr` of a `CalledProcessError` are `str` or `bytes` as the `run()` calls of the same `try` captured them; those of `TimeoutExpired` (bytes or `None` whatever `text=` says in CPython) are refused. | Static typing of attributes. | T: subprocess |
| A26 | The child is started by `fork` + `execve` with CPython's rules (PATH of the `env=` given, the first exec error reported, `cwd` failure named after the directory, `close_fds`, `SIGPIPE` restored); the parent's pipe feeding ignores `SIGPIPE` while it runs. `vfork`/`posix_spawn` are not used, so a parent with a very large memory spawns more slowly than CPython 3.12. A timeout kills the child with `SIGKILL` and waits for it, as CPython does; grandchildren are not killed. `sys.stdout.flush()` is a no-op elsewhere (stdout is flushed at exit) but flushes for real in a program that imports `subprocess`, so that the order against the child's output is the one CPython gives. `TimeoutExpired.timeout` is the number the call gave, or (a wait without pipes) the time that was left, as in CPython. | Fidelity of the error stages; speed left for later. | T: subprocess |
| A27 | Windows: `CreateProcessW` with `subprocess.list2cmdline` (tested bit for bit against CPython on Linux), inheritable pipe ends, one reader thread per output pipe and a writer thread for `input=`; a timeout calls `TerminateProcess` (exit code 1, as `Popen.kill`). OS errors are `[WinError n] text` (FormatMessage) mapped to `FileNotFoundError` / `PermissionError` / `NotADirectoryError`. Compiled with `--target windows` and not run here (stage G6 runs it on a Windows runner). | No Windows machine in this stage. | C: `ailproc_core.h` |
| A23 | Windows: `os.path` is CPython's ntpath logic (the Python code, not the C `_path_normpath` CPython uses on Windows, which differs for a few odd paths such as `\\\\?\\` prefixes); OS error numbers and text come from the C runtime, where CPython reports WinError codes for `os.listdir`, `os.mkdir` and friends. `open()` errors are errno based in both. | Not run here: built with `--target windows` and compared with the `ntpath` module on Linux. | T: files |
| A28 | The wall clock is a program input: `time.time()`, `time.time_ns()`, `time.sleep()`, `time.localtime/gmtime/strftime/mktime`, `datetime.now/utcnow/today/fromtimestamp/utcfromtimestamp`, `date.today/fromtimestamp` and `datetime.timestamp()` read it. `AIL_CLOCK=<seconds>[.<fraction>]` pins it for tests: every read returns that value (nine fraction digits are kept), and `sleep(x)` advances it by `x` (rounded away from zero to a nanosecond) without sleeping. CPython has no such switch, so differential tests give the CPython side the same value by patching `time.time/time_ns/sleep`, the "now" forms of `localtime/gmtime/strftime` and `datetime.now/utcnow` (`test_ail_clock.py` has the prelude). A malformed `AIL_CLOCK` is a `ValueError` at the first read. Local time is the OS time zone read the way CPython reads it (`tzset()`, then `localtime_r`; the `TZ` variable is respected). | Q7: a deterministic program is one whose inputs are fixed, and the clock is an input. | GENERAL_PURPOSE Q7, T: clock |
| A29 | Clock-related values differ from CPython's in these small ways: (a) `time.localtime()` and `gmtime()` return a class with the attributes `tm_year ... tm_isdst, tm_zone, tm_gmtoff` and nothing else (no indexing, no unpacking, no `print`); `time.struct_time(...)`, `asctime`, `ctime`, `tzset`, `altzone`, `daylight`, `timezone`, `tzname` are refused; `time.strftime(fmt, t)` takes a value from `localtime`/`gmtime`, not a tuple, and a year outside 1..9999 is a `RuntimeError`; `mktime` takes such a value or a 9-tuple written out. (b) A timestamp argument is converted to `float` first, so an `int` above 2**53 loses digits. (c) `fold` is kept only as far as `fromtimestamp()`/`now()` followed by `timestamp()` needs it: there is no `.fold` attribute, `repr()` of a `fold=1` value omits `fold=1`, and `replace()` or arithmetic on it gives `fold=0` (CPython's `replace` keeps it). (d) An out-of-range timestamp raises the CPython class with CPython's message (`OverflowError`, `ValueError: year N is out of range`, `OSError: [Errno 75] ...`), but the `OSError` has no `errno` attribute. (e) `time.sleep(x)` used as a value is the int 0, not `None`. | Smaller types than CPython's, no new exception classes. | R, T: clock |
| A30 | Time zones are fixed offsets only: `timezone.utc`, `UTC`, `timezone(timedelta, name)` (offsets in whole seconds). Aware datetimes carry the offset and the name; arithmetic, comparison, sorting, `isoformat`, `str`, `repr`, `strftime` (`%z`, `%Z`), `timestamp()`, `astimezone([tz])` (no argument: the OS zone as a fixed offset named by its abbreviation), `replace(tzinfo=)`, `utcoffset()`, `tzname()`, `now(tz)`, `fromtimestamp(t, tz)` and `fromisoformat` with `Z` or an offset agree with CPython. Differences: mixing naive and aware values in `<`, `-` and so on is a `RuntimeError` with CPython's `TypeError` text; `utcoffset()` and `tzname()` of a naive value (CPython: `None`) are a `RuntimeError`, and `d.tzinfo` can only be tested with `is None` / `is not None` (as a value it is refused); an offset with microseconds, `timezone.min/max`, an aware `time`, `strptime` with `%z`, `dst()`, `fold`, custom `tzinfo` subclasses and `zoneinfo` (it needs a tz database) are refused by name. | Smaller types than CPython's; the tz database is not built. | R, T: clock |
| A31 | `pathlib`: `iterdir()`, `glob()` and `rglob()` read the whole directory tree when they are called (CPython yields lazily, one `scandir` at a time), and they are accepted only where an iterator and a list behave alike (a `for` loop, a comprehension, `list() sorted() enumerate() zip() any() all() sum()`). The difference shows only when the tree changes while it is being walked, or when the walk is cut short by `break`/`any()` and a later directory is unreadable. Errors (a missing directory, a bad pattern) are raised at the call, which is the first iteration in every accepted form. | Lists, no generators on the typed path. | T: pathlib |
| A32 | `pathlib`: `p.parts` is a list inside the program (a tuple in CPython). It is built only where the two behave alike (iteration, one index, `len`, `list()`, `sorted()`, `in`, `"/".join`); anything that would show the type (`print(p.parts)`, slices, `tuple()`, unpacking) is refused (B). | No variable-length tuples on the typed path. | T: pathlib |
| A33 | `pathlib`: two exception classes differ in name. `glob("/abs")` raises `RuntimeError("Non-relative patterns are unsupported")` (CPython: `NotImplementedError`, a subclass of `RuntimeError`, so `except RuntimeError` catches both and `except NotImplementedError` is not built). CPython 3.12.3's `glob("./")` (and any pattern made of `.` parts and a trailing `/`) fails with an `AttributeError` that is a bug of that release; AIL raises `RuntimeError` with the same message. | `NotImplementedError` and `AttributeError` are not built exception classes. | T: pathlib |
| A34 | `pathlib`: `repr(p)` and the quoting in the messages of OS errors and `ValueError`s show non-ASCII characters raw when they are printable. The printable test is the C runtime's list of the common cases (C1 controls, no-break and zero-width spaces, line and paragraph separators, private use, specials), not the full Unicode database, so a rare unassigned code point can be shown raw where CPython writes `\uXXXX`. | Same C quoting as the file layer (`ailf_repr_str`). | C: `ailfile_core.h` |
| A35 | `pathlib`: `mkdir` raises a failure that has to propagate by repeating the OS call (a bare `raise` inside a handler is not built on the general path), so a directory created or removed by another process between the two calls can change which error is reported. Only programs that import pathlib get the exact `FileNotFoundError` handler (the wider rule of the typed path, section 5.1, stays for the others). | Handler semantics of the general runtime. | T: pathlib |
| A36 | `OSError(errno, strerror)` (G4: an `@extern` with `errno=True` raises it, exactly as the ctypes shim does) has `.errno`, `.strerror`, `str(e) == "[Errno N] text"` and the subclass CPython picks for the errno (ENOENT FileNotFoundError, EACCES/EPERM PermissionError, EISDIR, EEXIST, ENOTDIR; the other subclasses CPython has are not names AIL knows). `e.errno` and `e.strerror` are built only on a name bound by `except OSError as e` (or one of those five classes) and only on the typed path; on any other exception class they are refused by name, and the general path refuses `OSError(n, text)`. `str(e)` is built for every class (A45). An OSError that has no errno (a bare `OSError("x")`, or one the runtime raised for a file or a directory) raises `RuntimeError` when `.errno` or `.strerror` is read, where CPython gives `None` or the real number; `str(e)` of the runtime's file errors is the text it always had. | The typed exception is a code and a message; the errno is stored beside it only when the program builds `OSError(errno, text)`. | R, T: ffi, excfix |
| A37 | Windows text mode, checked by reading and by a cross build, not run (no Windows here): `print()`, `sys.stderr` and text files opened with the default `newline` write `\r\n` for each `\n`; `newline=""` and `"\n"` write nothing extra and `"\r"` / `"\r\n"` translate, as `io.TextIOWrapper` does; binary files and `sys.stdout.buffer` are untouched. The file layer opens every file `_O_BINARY` and translates itself (`ailfile_core.h`, `wnl`), stdout/stderr are left in the C runtime's text mode (`pyc.py` sets it) and switched to binary for byte writes. `test_ail_platforms.test_text_output_and_files_have_the_line_ends_cpython_writes_here` compares the bytes with CPython's own on the same platform, so the public Windows CI decides. | Same observable bytes as CPython 3.12 on Windows. | T: platforms |
| A38 | Threads (stage G12): the interpreter lock changes hands only at a loop back-edge (after another thread has waited 5 ms), in a wait (`join`, `acquire`, `Queue.get/put`, `Event.wait`, `Condition.wait`, `Future.result`) and around `time.sleep`. So `counter += 1` from several threads without a lock never loses an update, where CPython 3.12 may; file I/O, `subprocess.run`, sockets and stdin keep the lock while they block (no overlap, the same results). A program that imports `threading`, `queue` or `concurrent.futures` is built by the general path only, except the lock-free typed shapes of A66 and A67 (about 20 times the instructions of the typed path on a CPU loop, a quarter of CPython's). | Owner decision Q9 (GIL semantics). The typed runtime is not thread-aware before IR stages S5/S6. | GENERAL_PURPOSE Q9, design G12_THREADS.md, T: threads |
| A39 | `executor.map(...)` returns a list and `as_completed(fs)` returns, once every future is done, a list in completion order (CPython: generators). Iterating them, `list()`, `sorted()` give the same values; a loop body that runs while later futures are still pending runs later here. `threading.get_ident()` / `native_id` are small numbers (1 for the main thread), not the OS thread id. | No generators of this kind in the general runtime; the OS id is not reproducible anyway. | T: threads |
| A40 | An exception that escapes a thread prints `Exception in thread NAME:`, a placeholder for the traceback and `Type: message` on stderr (A4); `sys.exit()` in a thread ends the thread silently, as in CPython. Inside a class body of the general path `self.m()` calls the method of the class it is written in, not an override in a subclass (pyboxed2 binds it statically); a `Thread` subclass's `run()` is called through the object and is unaffected. | Stderr is outside the promise; the static binding is a general-path limitation (C24). | T: threads |
| A41 | `sqlite3`: the shape of the rows is declared by the program, in annotations CPython ignores (`rows: list[tuple[int, str]] = cur.fetchall()`). The values are checked when a row is read: a column of another SQLite type than declared (`TEXT` in an `int` column, an `INTEGER` in a `float` column), a NULL in a column declared without `Optional`, or a different number of columns raises `RuntimeError` with a message that names the column; CPython hands the value over whatever it is. A row with a nullable or a `bytes` column is a generated class, not a tuple: `row == (1, None)`, `sorted(rows)`, `tuple(row)`, slicing it and keeping it as a dict key are not built. | There is no `Any` and no `None` or `bytes` inside a tuple on the typed path; a wrong declaration must stop, not print another type. | T: sqlite3 |
| A42 | `sqlite3`: `cursor.lastrowid` is `None` in CPython on a cursor that has not run `execute` (a new cursor, or one that only ran `executemany` / `executescript`). It is an `int` here: `print(cur.lastrowid)`, `f"{cur.lastrowid}"`, `cur.lastrowid is None` and `is not None` give CPython's answer, and any other read of a `None` one raises `RuntimeError`. | There is no `None` for `int` values. | T: sqlite3 |
| A43 | `sqlite3`: a cursor loop (`for row in conn.execute(...)`) steps the statement lazily as CPython does, but `executemany(sql, [display])` evaluates the expressions of each row just before that row runs (CPython builds the whole list first), so an exception raised by the expressions of a later row happens after the earlier rows were inserted; a non-display `rows` argument is built first. The cursor of `conn.execute(...)` that the program does not keep is freed at once; a cursor held in a variable lives to the end of the program. Row values of a statement that returns text must be valid UTF-8 (otherwise `OperationalError: Could not decode to UTF-8 column ...`, CPython's class, with the text shown with replacement characters). `MemoryError` is a `RuntimeError`. | Statement-level lowering, no references to count. | T: sqlite3 |
| A44 | `sqlite3`: the lowering reads the program text before the tuple and Optional passes, and the C compiler then sees the rewritten text: a refusal of a later pass names a line of the rewritten program (`AIL_SQL_DUMP=1` prints it), not of the source. Refusals of the sqlite3 lowering itself name the source line. | `ast.unparse` does not keep line numbers (stdlib4 has the same cost). | C: `sqllower.py` |
| A45 | Exception values on the typed path (S4e): `str(e)`, `repr(e)`, `print(e)`, `f"{e}"`, `f"{e!r}"`, `"%s" % e`, `"{}".format(e)`, `e.args`, `len(e.args)`, `e.args[i]`, `type(e).__name__`, `type(e) is X`, `isinstance(e, X)` on a name bound by `except X as e`, for every exception class the typed runtime has, built with 0 to 8 arguments of str, int, float, bool or None (CPython's rules: no argument gives an empty string, one gives its str, several give the repr of the tuple, a KeyError gives the repr of its key; `OSError(errno, strerror[, filename])` gives `[Errno N] text: 'file'` and picks the subclass for the errno). Differences: `e.args[i]` is a str, so a non-str argument reads as its text (arithmetic on it is a compile-time refusal; `int(e.args[1])` works); `repr(e)` and `e.args` of `subprocess.CalledProcessError` and `TimeoutExpired` carry the message only (their `str(e)` is exact); `type(e)` and `e.args` are built only on the name bound by the handler, not on an exception held elsewhere; `e.args[1:]` is refused; `e.filename` is built on `except OSError`-family names for an exception raised for a file (or built with a filename) and raises `RuntimeError` where CPython gives `None`; `UnicodeDecodeError` has CPython's `str(e)`, `repr(e)` and `e.args`, but the position in a text file read through a chunked decoder is relative to the whole file where CPython's is relative to its 8 KiB chunk. `Exception` is a class (code 28): `raise Exception("m")` and `except Exception as e` take every exception that can be caught (a trap that stays uncatchable, such as running out of memory, still ends the program). User exception classes (`class E(Base)` with `Base` Exception, ValueError, ZeroDivisionError, IndexError, EOFError, RuntimeError, AssertionError, OverflowError or such a class) are built when the body is a docstring, `pass`, constants (`status = 404`), an `__init__` and a `__str__`: `__init__(self, a: int, b: str = "x")` with `super().__init__(...)` (or `Base.__init__(self, ...)`) once and `self.attr = <expression over the parameters>` stores, `__str__` returning an expression over `self.attr`. The kinds of the parameters come from annotations, defaults and the kinds of the constructor calls; a parameter whose kind cannot be told leaves the class as a class, and the program is refused (or taken by the general path). Attributes are read only on a handler name that names the class (`except NotFound as e: e.key`); an instance that lacks the attribute raises `RuntimeError` where CPython raises `AttributeError`; attributes cannot be assigned after construction; `__str__` is computed when the exception is built. A class-body constant is an attribute of every instance unless `__init__` stores the same name. Other methods, mutable class attributes, several bases, decorators and bases outside the list leave the class a class (refused here). | An exception is a code, a message, its arguments and, for a user class, an attribute table; a class is a code and a constructor function. | R, T: exc_str |
| A46 | `urllib.request` on the wire: the request is CPython's (`Python-urllib/3.12`, `Accept-Encoding: identity`, `Connection: close`, the names title-cased, `Content-Length: 0` for a bodiless POST/PUT/PATCH, byte for byte in the 23 shapes of `test_ail_http`) except that libcurl always writes `Host` right after the request line (http.client writes it after `Accept-Encoding`), and a header with an empty value goes out as `Name:` (http.client: `Name: `). The proxy comes from libcurl's reading of `http_proxy`, `https_proxy`, `all_proxy`, `no_proxy` (the same variables urllib reads, libcurl's matching rules). | Header order inside libcurl; the rest is the same text. | T: http |
| A47 | `urlopen` reads the whole response body before it returns (a `read()` of the response is then a copy). Memory: the body is held whole. | Streaming bodies need the file objects of stage G10. | T: http |
| A48 | `URLError.reason` is text here: `[Errno 111] Connection refused` (the errno libcurl reports), `timed out`, `unknown url type: ftp`, `no host given`, `[SSL: CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED] certificate verify failed: <OpenSSL's words> (_ssl.c:1006)`. CPython has an exception object (`print(e.reason)` and `str(e.reason)` agree; `e.reason.errno` and `isinstance(e.reason, ...)` are not built). A name that does not resolve is always `[Errno -2] Name or service not known` (libcurl does not say which `gai_strerror`). The text of other TLS failures is libcurl's. | A50 | The response is a class of the prelude: `r.status r.reason r.url r.code r.msg r.read() r.read(n) r.close() r.getcode() r.geturl() r.getheader(name, default)` (every header of that name joined with `, `, as CPython), `r.headers.get(name, default)` (the first one), `name in r.headers`, `with`. | A72 | `str` or `bytes` is decided by the text of the program where one name takes both: `base64.b64decode` and `hmac.compare_digest` take the `str` form when an argument is a str literal, an f-string, a `.decode()` / `str()` / `.hexdigest()` call or a `+` with one; an argument known only through a variable is taken as `bytes` (the typed path then refuses the program because a `str` is not `bytes`: write `s.encode("ascii")`). `secrets.choice(x)` reads `x` twice, so `x` has to be a name, attribute, subscript, literal or arithmetic of those, never a call. | A62 | With `--strict` / `AIL_STRICT=1` a program that only the general path accepts is refused at compile time with every blocker listed (exit 3); the default build still falls back and prints one `note:` line. | Strict mode promises typed-path speed, so it never builds on the general path. | T: strict |
| A65 | (IR default, 2026-10-19) The IR is the default; `AIL_IR=0` is the legacy emitter. | A66 | Typed parallelism (`typedpar.py`): `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=k).map(f, ...)` and `multiprocessing.Pool(k).map(f, ...)` over a function of int / float that only computes run `f` on at most k threads of one process, without a lock, and give a list in input order (CPython's `executor.map` gives an iterator; `Pool` uses k processes). The whole map runs where it is called, before the code that uses it: in `for v in ex.map(f, xs): print(v)`, an exception raised by `f` at some item (the first one in input order, with CPython's class and message) is raised before anything is printed, where CPython prints the results before that item first. `Pool(k).map(f, xs, chunksize)` ignores `chunksize`, and nothing is pickled. | No generators on the typed path; the results are the same values in the same order. | T: typed_parallel, design TYPED_PARALLEL.md |
| A67 | Typed scoped threads: `Thread(target=w, args=...)` made over `range(n)`, started and joined in one block, run as one parallel region of at most n threads (the pool's threads, not n new OS threads). | A68 | The typed parallel lowering rewrites the program text before the other passes: a later refusal of a program that uses `ThreadPoolExecutor.map`, `Pool.map` or scoped threads names a line of the rewritten program, as for sqlite3 (A44). A program the typed path refuses for another reason runs on the general path with the lowered map or threads as the plain loop they stand for (one thread; the same results). | `ast.unparse` does not keep line numbers. | C: `typedpar.py` |
| A74 | `ex.submit(f, ...)` and `Future.result()` on the typed path (lowered as a map; see TYPED_PARALLEL 5.1): all tasks of a list of futures, or of consecutive `f1 = ex.submit(f, ...)` statements, run as one parallel region where they are submitted, and `as_completed(futs)` yields the futures in submission order (one of CPython's completion orders). A future is used only as `fu.result()`. If a task raised, each `.result()` runs its task again on the calling thread in order, so the exception comes out at the `.result()` where CPython raises it, after the earlier results were used (a pure task: the repeat is unobservable). A future never read is not run again and its exception is dropped, as in CPython. | A race-free region has one outcome; the repeat keeps the exception where CPython raises it without per-task exception machinery in a worker. | T: typed_submit |
| A75 | Typed `ThreadPoolExecutor.map` / `Pool.map` accept a function over tuples of ints or of floats (arguments read as `t[i]` or unpacked, result `return (a, b)`); the worker runs on scalars and the result tuples are rebuilt after the region. `for t in ex.map(f, xs):` runs the loop body for the results that precede a failing item, then raises the exception at its item, as CPython does (the loop calls `f` itself once the region has raised); a comprehension over `ex.map` whose element expression calls anything but a builtin is refused (its effects would come before an exception). | A parallel region has one outcome; the exception must come where the sequential program raises it. | T: typed_tuples |

## B. Refusals that stand in for a difference

These are refused because the honest answer would differ from CPython or depend on CPython
internals:

- `is` / `is not` on `str`/`bytes` (`comparison Is`), and `hash()` as a value. CPython's answers
  depend on interning and hash randomization. Owner decision IR_S4 Q2/Q3. P
- Observable sharing of a container on the Python surface where the typed path would copy, e.g.
  ``line 3: `ys = xs` makes a second name for the same container ...`` (`aliasrefuse.py`). With the
  general path on, such a program is rebuilt on the general path, which shares. P
- Import orders whose result depends on when a module is imported (G1/G2). CPython binds a submodule on its package only when the submodule is imported, so these differ and are refused by name: reading `pkg.a` before the import that binds it (at module level, in a class body, or inside a function defined above the import, which may run first); a package that binds a name (`a = 5`, `def a`, `from .x import a`) that is also the name of a submodule the program imports (the import stores the module over the attribute); a top-level `import X` / `from X import ...` of a module that is neither in the standard library nor on the search path (CPython stops with `ModuleNotFoundError`, even when the name is never used). T: modules
- `main()` defined and never called. AIL's entry convention would run it, and CPython runs
  nothing. T: python_syntax
- Unseeded `random` and looping over a `defaultdict(set)` row: the output is not reproducible, or
  CPython's order is unspecified. R. (`time.time()`, `time.sleep()` and `datetime.now()` are built since
  stage G9: the clock is an input, like stdin; see A24.)
- A loop that changes the `Counter`/`defaultdict`/tuple-keyed dict it walks (CPython raises
  `RuntimeError`). R. A plain dict and a set raise the same error (C4, fixed).
- `int | None` and `d.get(k)` with one argument. There is no `None` for non-class types. P, R
- `open()` with a mode that is not a string literal (the file kind has to be known at build time),
  an `encoding=` other than utf-8, ascii, latin-1 (utf-8-sig, utf-16, cp932 ...), an `errors=` other than
  strict, replace, ignore, `closefd=False`, `opener=`, a file name that is not a `str`; `os.walk` with
  `topdown=False` / `followlinks`, `os.scandir`, `os.stat`, `os.environ.get(key)` without a default
  (there is no `None` str) and iterating `os.environ`. T: files
- `subprocess`: `shell=True`, a `str` command, `Popen`, `call`, `check_call`, `getoutput`, `stdin=`/`stdout=` given a file object,
  `bufsize=`, `executable=`, `preexec_fn=`, `pass_fds=`, `start_new_session=`, `user=`/`group=`, `startupinfo=`/`creationflags=`;
  `text=` / `capture_output=` that is not `True`/`False` written out (str or bytes has to be known when the program is built); an
  `encoding=` other than utf-8, ascii, latin-1; `env={**os.environ}` (`os.environ` cannot be copied or iterated); constructing
  `CompletedProcess` / `CalledProcessError` / `TimeoutExpired`; an exception attribute that would be `None`; bytes results on the
  general path. T: subprocess
- `pathlib`: `PurePath`, `PurePosixPath`, `PureWindowsPath`, `PosixPath`, `WindowsPath`; a Windows target (the POSIX flavour only is
  built); `stat lstat touch chmod home expanduser relative_to is_relative_to is_symlink samefile walk match parents readlink
  symlink_to hardlink_to owner group as_uri with_segments`; `resolve(strict=True)`, `glob(case_sensitive=)`,
  `exists(follow_symlinks=)`; a path as a dict key, set member or subscript, `<` `>` `<=` `>=` `in` on paths, `p == "text"`,
  `%` formatting and format specs; `p.parts` outside the places of A32, the iterator forms of A31 used as values (`it = p.iterdir()`,
  `next()`, `print(p.glob("*"))`), `min/max/set/tuple/dict` of paths; `isinstance(x, Path)` and the class `Path` used as a value; a
  path passed to a parameter, or returned from a function, whose annotation does not say `Path` / `list[Path]`. T: pathlib
- Threads (stage G12): a lambda or a function defined inside a function as the function of a thread or an executor
  (the thread prelude takes a function by name), `threading.local Timer Barrier excepthook settrace setprofile
  stack_size ThreadError`, `queue.PriorityQueue`, `ProcessPoolExecutor`, `concurrent.futures.wait`,
  `Condition.wait_for`, `Future.add_done_callback`, the module as a value, an import inside a function,
  `from threading import *`, `executor.map` over three or more iterables, `kwargs=` that is not a dict display. T: threads
- Typed parallelism without a lock (`typedpar.py`, TYPED_PARALLEL.md): a map whose function is not a module-level def over
  int / float that only computes (I/O, `global`, lists, str, recursion), the iterator of `executor.map` used as a value,
  `submit` / `Future`, a thread function that writes anything but `out[k]` (k its thread index) or appends, threads not
  joined in the block that starts them, `Lock` / `Queue` / any other name of the three modules. Each is a note with a
  `write instead:` line; the program is then built on the general path under its lock (G12), and refused with
  `AIL_NO_GENERAL=1`. T: typed_parallel
- `sqlite3`: `row_factory` / `sqlite3.Row`, `register_adapter`, `register_converter`, `detect_types=`, `create_function` /
  `create_aggregate` / `create_window_function` / `create_collation` / `set_authorizer` / the progress and trace callbacks,
  `fetchmany`, `cursor.description` / `arraysize` / `connection`, `iterdump`, `backup`, `blobopen`, `serialize`, `enable_load_extension`,
  `connect(timeout=, uri=, check_same_thread=, factory=, cached_statements=, autocommit=)`, an `isolation_level` that is not a literal
  or is set after `connect`, `sqlite3.version` / `sqlite_version` / `complete_statement` / `Warning` / the `SQLITE_` constants,
  `sqlite_errorcode` / `sqlite_errorname`, `executemany` with named parameters or inside an expression, parameters that are a call result
  or are held in a variable beside a SQL text that is not a literal, `*` in a parameter list, `with conn:` whose body has `return`,
  `break` or `continue`, `fetchone()` / `fetchall()` / a loop over a cursor whose row shape is not declared, `import sqlite3` inside a
  function, a cross build (`--target`) and the general path. T: sqlite3
- `urllib`: anything but `urlopen(url or Request, data=, timeout=)`, `Request(url, data=, headers=, method=)` and their results
  (`build_opener install_opener urlretrieve` the handler classes `ProxyHandler HTTPBasicAuthHandler HTTPCookieProcessor ...`, `http.client`,
  `urlopen(context=/cafile=/capath=)`, `data=` on `urlopen(request)`); `ftp:`, `file:` and `data:` URLs written out; `r.info() r.readline()
  r.readlines() r.readinto() r.peek() r.fileno()`, iterating a response, `r.headers` as a value, `r.headers["X"]`, `headers.get(name)` and
  `getheader(name)` with no default, `r.headers.items() keys() get_all() get_content_type() get_content_charset()`; `req.headers`,
  assigning `req.data` / `req.full_url`; `HTTPError(...)` / `URLError(...)` constructed, `e.args`, `e` passed on, an exception
  attribute outside the list of A49, `e.code` in a handler that names `URLError` only; `urllib.parse.urlparse urlsplit urlunparse
  urlunsplit urldefrag parse_qs parse_qsl quote_from_bytes unquote_to_bytes`, `quote(bytes)`, `encoding=`/`errors=`/`doseq=`/`quote_via=`
  arguments; `urlencode` of anything but a dict with `str` keys and values (a number or bool written out is `str()`-ed as CPython
  does); the general path (`urllib is built on the typed path only`), a cross target (`AIL_TARGET`); libcurl's header missing (the message
  gives the install line for apt, dnf, brew and MSYS2). T: http
- Library batch 5: `decimal` (needs decimal arithmetic with a context); `Fraction.from_decimal`, `format(Fraction, spec)` / `f"{x:.2f}"` of a
  Fraction, `divmod(fraction, x)`; `enum.Flag` / `IntFlag`, the functional API `Enum("Color", "RED GREEN")`, mixed-in bases (`class C(str, Enum)`),
  `__init__` / `__new__` / `_generate_next_value_` / class methods in an enum; `namedtuple(rename=, module=)`, `__new__`, class attributes in a
  NamedTuple class, `typing.NamedTuple("P", [...])`; `random.Random()` without a seed, a str / bytes / float / None seed, `random.SystemRandom`,
  `getstate` / `setstate`, `randbytes`, the other distributions, `random.<function>` used as a value next to `random.Random`; `calendar.calendar` /
  `prcal` / the Calendar classes / `setfirstweekday` / `timegm`; `array('f' | 'u')`, `tobytes` / `frombytes` / `tofile`; `io.BytesIO` and the rest of `io`;
  `statistics.mean / stdev / variance` of floats (CPython sums exact fractions of floats, which need integers beyond 64 bits; `fmean` and the int forms are built).
  T: stdlib5
- Mutable defaults (`def f(acc: list[int] = [])`) are refused on the typed path. P (typed). The
  general path shares one default between calls like CPython (C3, fixed).
- Typed-path inheritance (`docs/design/TYPED_INHERITANCE.md`): a call through a base class that can reach
  a stub method (`...`, `pass`, `raise NotImplementedError`) of a class the program constructs (CPython
  returns `None` or raises); a method or field that only some subclasses have, used through the base
  outside `if isinstance(x, C):` (CPython raises `AttributeError` for the others); a subclass `__init__`
  that leaves a base field unset or reads one before `super().__init__` (`AttributeError`); assigning a
  class attribute. Overrides must keep the parameter count and the declared types of the method they
  override. T: typed_inherit
- Special methods on the typed path (AIL_SPEC 2.3a): an iterator class (`__next__`), `iter()`/`next()` of an
  instance, `hash()` of an instance, an instance of a class that compares by value as a set element or dict
  key, `x == None` on a class with `__eq__`, a slice of `__getitem__`/`__setitem__`, a format spec on an
  instance (`__format__`), `__exit__` reading the exception value, `return`/`try`/`with` inside a generator
  `__iter__`, a property setter, `cls` as a value, `str.format` / `%` whose later arguments call functions,
  and a special method on a class with a base class. T: dunders

- Nested functions, closures, `match`, chained raises (stage nested-fn): reading `__cause__`, `__context__`,
  `__suppress_context__` or `__traceback__` (A53); a bare `except:` in a program that calls `sys.exit()` (A54); a `match`
  that is left as written -- a class pattern of a class the program does not define (`case Fraction(1, 2)`), positional
  sub-patterns of a class without `__match_args__` (or a dataclass), an or-pattern whose alternatives bind different names,
  a sequence pattern in a program that uses `collections.deque` (A55); keyword or `*` arguments in a call through a
  function value, a function value with `*args` / `**kwargs` / keyword-only parameters (A56); on the typed path, a
  name a nested function changes or rebinds whose type cannot be told from the program (annotate it where it is first
  assigned), and a container kind a class field cannot hold (`list[tuple[...]]`, `list[set[int]]`, `dict[K, list[V]]`) --
  the default build takes those on the general path. T: nested_functions, match, raise_from
## C. Bugs (found 2026-10-17; none is documented elsewhere unless noted)

| # | Kind | Program | AIL | CPython | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | silent | `sum([0.1] * 10)` | `0.9999999999999999` | `1.0` | CPython 3.12 `sum()` of floats uses compensated (Neumaier) summation. AIL adds naively. P (typed) **Fixed** (branch specfix-num). Float `sum()` runs CPython 3.12's `builtin_sum_impl` algorithm bit for bit: Neumaier compensation for float items, plain addition for the first item after an int prefix and for int items inside the float run, compensation dropped when a non-number ends the run, and the final `c` rule. Typed path: runtime part `FSUM` (`ail_fsum_add`/`ail_fsum_end`); both general runtimes: `ail_sum_step`. The start value and int/float mixes follow CPython (general path). Found on the way and fixed: `sum(xs[:k])` over a slice of a `list[float]` summed the truncated ints on the typed path (silent); the typed path now refuses it by name and the general path answers. `math.fsum` is not built (refused by name); the unused fsum helper in the old judge runtime was brought to CPython's algorithm. Tests: `test_ail_spec_fixes` (random floats). |
| C2 | **fixed** | (fixed on branch specfix-alias: `aliasrefuse.py` now scans every shape below and refuses it by name on the typed path, so the program is rebuilt on the general path, which shares; tests in `test_ail_fuzz_findings.py`: `SHARE`, `SHARE_FINE`) Python surface, typed path: `g.append(xs); xs.append(3); len(g[0])`; `b = Bag(ys); ys.append(8); len(b.xs)`; `zs = b.xs; zs.append(9); len(b.xs)`; also `g.append(xs)` inside a helper | `2`, `1`, `1` | `3`, `2`, `3` | Storing a list into a list of rows, passing one to a constructor, or binding a local to a field list copies it. `aliasrefuse.py` only scans `name = name` and `obj.f = name`. The reference documents this copy for the brace surface only. P (default build, because the typed path took it) |
| C3 | **fixed** | (the general path now evaluates a default that builds something, once, when the `def` runs, as CPython does) general path: `def add(x, acc: list[int] = []) -> int: acc.append(x); return len(acc)` called twice | `1 1` | `1 2` | The general path builds a fresh default on every call. The typed path refuses. P |
| C4 | **fixed** | (a loop over a dict or set raises catchable `RuntimeError("dictionary changed size during iteration")` / `"Set changed size during iteration"` at the next step when the size changed, typed and general path) `for k in d: d[k + 10] = 0` on a plain `dict` | never ends | `RuntimeError: dictionary changed size during iteration` | P (typed and default) |
| C5 | silent | `(-4.0) ** 0.5` (non-constant) | `nan` | `(1.2246467991473532e-16+2j)` | Should be refused or raise. AIL has no complex type. P (typed) **Fixed.** Constant operands (`(-4.0) ** 0.5`) are refused at compile time with `file:line:col` and the words "complex number"; any other operands stop the program at run time with "a negative number raised to a fractional power is a complex number in Python; AIL has no complex type" (uncatchable, never `nan`). |
| C6 | silent | `(10.0) ** 400` (non-constant) | `inf` | `OverflowError` | float `**` overflow is not checked. P (typed) **Fixed.** Float `**` is CPython's `float_pow`: a finite overflow raises `OverflowError` (`(34, 'Numerical result out of range')`, catchable), `0.0 ** negative` raises `ZeroDivisionError`. `FPOWCK`/`ail_fpow`; the C name `pow` is routed to it, so the IR emitter (which spells `pow(...)`) gets it too. |
| C7 | silent | `"a\x0bb\x1cc
d\x85e\r\nf".splitlines()` built at run time | 4 lines | 6 lines | Some of CPython's line boundaries (`\v \f \x1c-\x1e \x85 
 
`) are not split on. P **Fixed.** The typed `splitlines()` already knew every boundary; the general runtime now also splits at `\x85`, U+2028 and U+2029 (`keepends=True` exists on the general path only; the typed path refuses it by name). |
| C8 | loud | `int("1_000")`, `float("1_0.5")` | `ValueError` | `1000`, `10.5` | Underscores in numeric strings. P **Fixed.** `int(s)`, `int(s, base)` and `float(s)` follow CPython's grammar: single underscores between digits (also after a base prefix), rejecting `1__0`, `_1`, `1_`, `1_e5`, `1._5`. One shared parser (`NUMPARSE`, `ail__asc_to_int`/`ail__asc_to_float`) serves the typed and both general runtimes. Also corrected: `float()` no longer trims U+001C-U+001F (CPython does not), rejects `nan(...)`, and a malformed over-long literal is `ValueError`, not the overflow stop. |
| C9 | loud | `int("١٢")` (non-ASCII decimal digits) | `ValueError` | `12` | Known (IR_S4 Q4, decided: add a Unicode digit table). P **Fixed.** `int()` / `float()` read Unicode decimal digits (Nd, generated table `ail__nd` from `tools/gen_digits.py`) and turn other white space into a space exactly as `_PyUnicode_TransformDecimalAndSpaceToASCII` does. The typed path already had `isdigit` / `isdecimal` / `isnumeric` tables; the general runtime now has them for `isdigit()` (`isdecimal()` / `isnumeric()` stay refused by name there). |
| C10 | loud | `.upper()` / `.lower()` on a non-ASCII str built at run time (`"straße".upper()`) | `AIL internal error: .upper() reached a non-ASCII code point`, exit 1 | `STRASSE` | Literals are folded at compile time (test_ail_fuzz_findings). Run-time values are not. P **Fixed.** The general runtime's `lower()` / `upper()` map full Unicode (`ß` -> `SS`, final sigma) with the same generated tables as the typed path (`BOXCASE`, appended only to a program that calls them). The legacy v1 general runtime (`AIL_GENERAL=v1`) still stops on non-ASCII text. |
| C11 | loud | `int(float("inf"))` | `ValueError` | `OverflowError` | Wrong exception class. P **Fixed** on the typed and general paths: infinity is `OverflowError`, nan `ValueError`. **Open**: the IR emitter (`python/pkg-v12/ir/emit_c.py`, `int(float)` check) still raises `ValueError` for infinity; it is outside this change. |
| C12 | **fixed** | (messages now say `min() over str values is not built` and the alias refusal) `min("ab", "b")`; `e = d; e["b"] = 2` on a `dict[str, int]` | refused with `a str constant ... used where a number is needed` | runs | Misleading refusal. The first is a missing feature, and the second should be the alias refusal. P (typed) |
| C13 | message | `import regex` | `there are no regular expressions here at all` | — | Stale text: `re` is built. pyfunc.py l.1190 **Fixed.** `import regex` now says the third-party module is not built and points at `import re`. |
| C14 | message | `print(b"..")`, `Optional[int]` | refusal without `file:line:col` | — | P **Fixed** for `X | None` / `Optional[X]` on a non-struct type (typed path): the refusal names the annotation's line. Every other refusal checked (the specprobe set) already carried `file:line:col`. |
| C15 | not a bug (false report) | `add(acc, f[0], int(f[1]))` with `f[1]` raising: `f[0]` was reported empty after the exception | — | `bad b` | Could not be reproduced at 3729714 (AIL_IR=0 and 1); the report came from a stale binary. Kept as a guard: `test_str_element_survives_a_later_argument_raising`. |
| C17 | not reproduced | `list(names.keys())` on a 3-key `dict[str, int]` reported as length 1 | — | `3` | Several shapes (str and int keys, `.keys()/.values()/.items()`, `list(d)`, `sorted`) all match CPython at 3729714 on the typed and general path. Kept as a guard: `test_lists_made_from_dict_views_have_the_right_length`. |
| C16 | silent | `print(2.0 ** -1017)` and 45 other powers of two | more digits than CPython | CPython's shortest repr | From the json-perf work. **Fixed**: `ail_fmt_float` (typed and both general runtimes) tries the neighbours of the nearest decimal one digit shorter (`ail__fmt_shorten`), because just below a power of two the round-trip interval is half as wide on the low side. Tested on every power of two and random doubles. The Ryu formatter of the json-perf branch is not needed for correctness; reuse it for speed only. |
| C18 | **fixed** | (stage G7, branch g7-files) `open(p).read()` in text mode, file bytes `a\r\nb\rc\n` | `len` 7 | `len` 6 | CPython text files use universal newlines on every OS (`\r\n` and `\r` read as `\n`). `for line in open(p)` already agreed. `sys.stdin.read()` agrees too (7), because CPython does not translate stdin on POSIX. P (typed, fbbf1e7, 2026-10-18). **Fixed:** the whole-file reader `ail_file_read` translates `\r\n` and a lone `\r` to `\n` (typed C of every program that reads a file this way changes by those lines); every other text file goes through `ailfile_core.h`, which implements all `newline=` settings. Test: `test_ail_files` (`test_whole_file_read_uses_universal_newlines`, and the newline fuzz). |
| C19 | **fixed** | (general path) `def f(a, b): return a \| b` called with two sets; `a - b`, `a & b`, `a ^ b`, `a \|= b`, `a <= b` | `TypeError: unsupported operand type(s) for \|: 'set' and 'set'` | the new set | The operators dispatched on integers only unless both operands were known sets at build time. **Fixed**: they dispatch on the run-time kind (set / frozenset), the result has the type of the left operand, and the in-place forms change a set in place (a frozenset is rebound). |
| C20 | **fixed** | (general path) `frozenset([1, 2])`, `{frozenset([1]): 'a'}`, `isinstance(x, frozenset)` | `the name 'frozenset' is not a known function/class/builtin` | `frozenset({1, 2})` | Built: hashable (CPython's frozenset hash, so it is a dict key and a set element), `frozenset()` / `frozenset(it)`, repr `frozenset({...})` / `frozenset()`, `==` with a set, the read-only methods (a frozenset result stays a frozenset), `AttributeError: 'frozenset' object has no attribute 'add'` for the mutators. `x in S` and `S.discard(x)` with a `set` x use its frozenset, as CPython does. |
| C21 | **fixed** | (general path) `{1: 2} == {1: 2}`, `d1 == d2` | `False` | `True` | `==` on dicts always answered `False`. **Fixed**: key by key, order ignored. Dict iteration order was already insertion order (checked: delete and re-add, `popitem`, `update`, comprehensions). |
| C22 | **fixed** | general path: `def g(): try: raise ValueError("x")\n except ValueError: raise` called from `try: g() except ValueError as e: print(e)` | `ValueError: x` uncaught, exit 1 | prints `x` | A bare `raise` inside a handler does not re-raise the exception on the general path: the handler entry clears the pending exception (`ail_exc_clear` in `pyboxed2.stmt_Try`), so the `goto` to the enclosing handler carries no exception and the caller's `except` does not match. P (found in G10; the pathlib prelude and `_ail_makedirs` of G7 avoid or suffer it; `_ail_makedirs` re-raises with a bare `raise` and so misbehaves on the general path). **Fixed** (excfix): the handler keeps a copy of the pending exception in frame slots and a bare `raise` restores it (also in a `finally` that runs for an exception); `AIL_LIMIT` stops still run no handler or finally. `test_ail_excfix`. |
| C23 | **fixed** | typed: `try: os.rmdir(".")\n except FileNotFoundError: print("fnf")\n except OSError: print("os")` (any OS error that is not one of the five classes: `EINVAL`, `ENOTEMPTY`, `EXDEV` ...) | `fnf` | `os` | `except FileNotFoundError` also catches the plain `OSError` code on the typed path, because the older whole-file builtins (`read_bytes`, `file_size`, `open(p).read()`) raise `OSError` for a missing file (`pyfunc._handler_codes`). **Fixed for programs that import pathlib** (every file error there has its own code, `pathlower.ACTIVE`); other programs keep the wider rule. P **Fixed for all programs** (excfix): `except FileNotFoundError` takes only code 11; `except OSError` takes every OS-family code; the older whole-file builtins (`open().read()`, `read_bytes`, `file_size`, `list_dir`, the binary file handle) raise the class of their errno once the OS-family codes exist. Checked on the typed and the general path in `test_ail_excfix`. |
| C24 | **fixed** | `import pkg\ndef get(): return pkg.a.X\ntry: print(get())\nexcept AttributeError: print("AttributeError")\nimport pkg.a\nprint(get())` (`pkg/__init__.py` empty) | `NameError: name 'pkg__a__X' is not defined` uncaught, exit 1 | `AttributeError` then `1` | A function that reads a submodule attribute was accepted whenever it was defined, and run before the import it hit a global the merged program had not defined yet (loud, and the wrong class). **Fixed**: refused by name when the function is defined above the import that binds the submodule (`test_ail_modules.ImportOrder`). |
| C25 | **fixed** | `pkg/__init__.py`: `a = 5`; `main.py`: `import pkg; print(pkg.a); import pkg.a; print(pkg.a)` | `5` `5` (or a crash for `import pkg.a as x`) | `5` then `<module 'pkg.a'>` | `import pkg.a` stores the module over the attribute the package defined; the merged program kept the variable. **Fixed**: refused by name (`ail_modules._rebound_by_submodule`). |
| C26 | **fixed** | `import nothere` (or `import numpy as np`) that the program never uses | built, prints the program's output | `ModuleNotFoundError`, exit 1 | The general path ignored an unused import of a module that does not exist. **Fixed**: a top-level import of a module that is neither in the standard library nor local is refused at its line (`ail_chatgpt._check_importable`). An import inside `try:`, a function or `if` is not looked at. |
| C27 | **fixed** | (fixed on branch vdispatch: a call whose receiver is statically an instance of a class that a subclass overrides now tests the run-time class, most derived first, and calls that method; `pyboxed2._virtual_plan` / `_emit_virtual`, applied to `x.m()`, `self.m()` and the statically bound `==`, `<`, `in`, `[]`, `len`, `str`, `repr`; `super().m()` is bound from the owner's base, which is CPython's rule for single inheritance; a class whose methods nothing overrides keeps a direct call, no run-time test and no cost. Also fixed there: a class attribute the subclass does not redefine is now read through the base (`D().K` raised AttributeError). The typed path builds single inheritance since branch typed-inherit (`docs/design/TYPED_INHERITANCE.md`, `test_ail_typed_inherit.py`); multiple inheritance is refused by name on both paths. A special method that only a subclass defines is refused by name when the static class lacks it. Tests: `test_ail_virtual_dispatch.py`) (general path) `class A:` with `def f(self): return self.g()` and `def g(self): return 1`; `class B(A):` overriding `g` to return 2; `B().f()` | `1` | `2` | A method's `self.m()` is bound to the method's own class at build time (`pyboxed2._call_attribute`, `ctx.self_name`), not looked up on the run-time class. Found in G12 (the thread prelude's `LifoQueue` first overrode `_get`; it now uses a flag). Also: a user method named like a dict/str method (`q.get()`, `t.join()`) whose call passes fewer arguments than the builtin of that name crashed the emitter with an `IndexError`; **fixed** in G12 (`_dynamic_user_method` raises `TypeError` at run time for a receiver that is not an instance). P |
| C28 | **fixed** | `class T:` with `def __init__(self) -> None: self.done = True`, then `print(T().done)` (also `self.done: bool = True`, `self.ok = n > f`, an unannotated method or function that returns a comparison, a list or dict filled with comparisons, `max`/`min` of bools, `a & b` of bools) | `1` | `True` | `boolkind` knew a field was a bool only through an `__init__` parameter annotated `bool`; a field assigned a bool literal or expression, an unannotated method result, an element of an inferred list or dict, `max(flags)` and `a & b` were ints and printed as ints. **Fixed** on branch boolfield: `boolkind` infers, to a fixed point over the program, which fields (every store to the name is a bool), results of functions and methods, parameters of functions, methods and constructors (every call passes a bool), lists and dicts of bools (`dict[K, bool]` is a new annotation) hold bools; the compiler's `_is_bool_expr` is now `boolkind.boolish` with real types. A slot that would hold a bool in some places and a number in others (a field, a result, a list or dict, a ternary, `max(x > 1, 0)`, a parameter declared `int` given a bool, a local that is printed) is refused by name, and the general path runs it. Arithmetic is unchanged (`True + 1 == 2`, `sum([True, True]) == 2`). Printing a whole list or dict is refused on the typed path as before. | C71 | loud, **fixed** | general path: `for i in range(2**63-1, -(2**63-1), -3074457345618258602): print(i)`; `len(range(...))`, `range(...)[i]` and `reversed(range(...))` with bounds near the int64 limits; `reversed(range(a, b))` and `x in range(a, b)` | the loop never ended (the next index, an int64 sum, wrapped round and stayed in range); `len` and indexing took `stop - start` in int64 (wrong values); `reversed()` and `in` raised `TypeError: 'range' object is not reversible` / `argument of type 'range' is not iterable` | 7 values; the exact lengths and items; `True` / `False` | Fixed (branch bugs7). The general runtime counts a range in uint64 (up to 2**64 - 1 items) and walks the iterator by that count, stepping modulo 2**64; indexing computes `start + step * ordinal` the same way, so every value CPython gives is given. Where CPython has a number AIL's int64 cannot hold the stop is the declared one (A1): `len()` of a range of more than 2**63 - 1 items (`len(range(-M, M))`) is an `OverflowError` in CPython too (`Python int too large to convert to C ssize_t`) and `integer overflow`, exit 1, here. The typed path already counted in uint64 (`ail_range_count`) and stops with `range length exceeds int64`. `x in range(...)` is arithmetic (an int, a bool, or a float that is a whole number in int64; any other kind is not a member). `test_ail_bugs7` `test_range_limits`, `test_range_views`. |

| C287 | loud, **fixed in B1** | **When a Python-syntax program gets exact ints.** An int operation is big-int capable unless its operands and result are proven locally, from the function itself, to fit int64. A function with any unproven int local is demoted by rule 4: it gets no typed int64 body or clone and runs with boxed exact ints on the general path. Local proofs (BIGINT.md rule 3): int literals; `len`, `ord`, `str.count`, `str.find`, `list.index`; `+ - *` whose bounds stay within ±2**62; `//` and `%` by a proven nonzero literal; comparisons; float-valued locals; loop counters from `range` whose bounds are proven. A parameter, attribute, container item or call result is unproven unless it is one of those builtins; `math.factorial/comb/perm/isqrt/gcd/lcm/prod/floor/ceil/trunc` calls are unproven even with constant arguments; `**` on a non-constant base is unproven. A program whose ints are all proven keeps the typed int64 path. | `def f(n): return n * n` then `print(f(3037000500))`; `math.comb(10, 3)` | before B1: `integer overflow`, exit 1 (default build) | `9223372037000250000`; `120` | A program that only the typed path can build (a binary file or a socket) falls back to it after the general path fails, with only the hard big-int constructs refused, so file programs keep building. Branch bigint B1. The old call-site/field/container scans were removed (`bigcap.py`, `biggate.py`, `pyboxed2.py`); `_math_call_fits` was removed. `test_ail_bigint` `MarkRules`, `DefaultBuild`, `test_b1_review_probes_on_both_builds`. |

| C290 | declared | **Which programs get the exact-int runtime (B1 local-proof gate).** A Python-syntax program gets the big-int runtime when its general-path code has an int arithmetic operator whose operands are int/bool or are not proven to be another kind. Only local evidence is used: a parameter, attribute, container item or module global read from a function is unknown (so it keeps the runtime); a float literal/local, a float-returning math call and a locally tracked float local are not int arithmetic; built-in bounded int calls (`len`, `ord`, `str.count/find`, `list.index`, `bit_length`) are known int. There is no call-site, class-field or container scan and no constant comb/perm exemption. A construct that makes a big int without an overflow (literal beyond int64, `int(str)`, `int.from_bytes`, factorial/comb/perm/isqrt/gcd/lcm/prod/floor/ceil/trunc, `**`/`<<`) also keeps it. B1b removed that int-free pre-gate (C227): a parameter, item or attribute that is not proven float is bigint-capable, so a float-only program reached through arguments or containers now takes the general path; only a program whose values the local proof covers keeps the typed path. A program whose ints are all locally proven is also unchanged; the rest may grow by the general-path runtime. B1 measurements: int probes i1–i5 (default build, base 38771a1) 16,424→90,816; 99,304→99,304; 121,960→121,640; 17,560→107,904; 111,912→111,912 B; forced-general base and current are identical (90,816 / 107,904 B for the two that moved), so the big runtime itself did not grow. Re-review (2026-10-24) int series: b1_probes 159,856→159,408; gate_math_constants 17,176→118,248; marked_transitive 144,208→144,208; general_unboxed_loud_stop 17,232→100,224; general_overflow_loop 143,656→143,656 B. `test_ail_bigint` `DefaultBuild`, `test_b1_review_probes_on_both_builds`. |

Constant math calls are now unproven, so `bigint_cases/gate_math_constants.py` takes the general path and grows to 118,248 B from 17,176 B (inside the B1 size allowance, over the old 25 KB note); it prints CPython's answer. A read that is definitely assigned is untouched, and a program without a possibly-unassigned read gets byte-identical C. Otherwise the variable gets a flag local `_ail_set_<name>` (False at entry, True after each binding statement) and each such read becomes `name if (_ail_set_name or _ail_unbound_name()) else name`, the generated helper raising the exception (the classes `NameError` and `UnboundLocalError` join the program as user exception classes, userexc.py; only in a program that needs a flag). A typed clone never carries a flag (it cannot raise into the boxed handler stack): `info['_mixed_clone']` makes `to_c` refuse such a function and its callers. Also fixed on the general path: `del x` of an unassigned local raised nothing, and `except NameError` did not take an `UnboundLocalError` (a program that names `NameError` in a handler gets the base-class line in its runtime; no other program's C changes). | C88 | speed, **changed** | general path: `re` (literal patterns) | same matches, groups and errors as before, the Pike VM and the byte DFA now in C (rebrt.py); the Python text of the engine stays in the program and answers when the C meets a Unicode predicate (\w \d \s or a class on a code point above 127) or a subject that is not a bytes object | same | Branch fastlib2: 40-50% of the instructions of re programs on the general path (up to 28x fewer instructions). Test: test_ail_fastlib2.FastLib2. |
| C89 | speed, **changed**; silent, **fixed** | general path: `statistics` (finite check, exact partial sums, sum of squared deviations, integer sums) | same results as before, the loops now in C (statbrt.py). Two answers now follow CPython: `fmean([-0.0])` is `0.0` (math.fsum drops a zero partial), and `harmonic_mean` returns 0 for a zero that comes before a negative value (negative before zero still raises) | same | Branch fastlib2. Test: test_ail_fastlib2.FastLib2. |
| C84 | loud (memory), **fixed** | general path run with a 16 KiB collection threshold under ASan (gdev, 267 programs that build with `--general-only`): `d.items()` (`ail_dict_items`), `enumerate`, `itertools.combinations` / `permutations`, `sorted` / `sort` with a lambda key returning a tuple, `.upper()` / `.lower()` on a long string in a lambda (`ail_boxed_case`), a `next()` on a generator-made tuple | 15 programs report a heap-use-after-free (g01_010, g01_016, g05_000, g05_002, g06_026, g06_034, g06_036, g06_038, g16_032, g17_000, g17_026, g17_036, g18_006, g24_004, g24_008) | the output | Fixed (branch gcroots): the runtime keeps every object it holds across a later allocation in a frame: `AIL_ROOT_N` / `AIL_ROOT1` register an existing local or array as a frame without copying. The sinks root their own arguments (`ail_tuple_new` its items, `ail__iter_wrap` its target and state, `ail_iter` a fresh iterable, `ail_str_buf1/2/3` the source strings of every str algorithm that reads them after allocating the result, `ail_list_sort` the key array); the builders root their result (dict.items, the itertools combinatorics, cycle/chain/zip/zip_longest/pairwise/islice/groupby, Counter, deque, heapq, copy.deepcopy, statistics, str.format/rsplit/partition, set helpers, list.index's message, ctx init and class creation). A float literal (`(1.5, 0.5)`, `math.pi`, `float()`) goes to a slot at once instead of an inline `ail_box_float` beside another allocation. Audit: every C function of the boxed runtime (cruntime OBJ2/BOXCASE, pyboxed2, safetyrt, threadrt, randrt, filert) with two allocating calls, or one in a loop, was read; a GC-stress build (`-DAIL_GC_STRESS`: a collection at every allocation) under ASan+UBSan runs the 426 programs of gdev and dev that build with  with no report, and the 16 KiB sweep is clean. `test_ail_gcstress` (registered in ail_check.py). |
| C85 | silent, **fixed** | general path: `a = [1]; h = [a, a]; h[1] += [3]; print(a)` (also `d["k"] += [4]`, `g[0] *= 2`, `s["a"] |= {2}`) | `[1]` (a new list is built and stored in `h[1]`) | `[1, 3]` (`list.__iadd__` extends in place) | Fixed (branch gcroots): an item target takes the in-place operator (`ail_op_iadd`, `ail_op_imul`, `ail_op_ibitor`, ...) as a name and an attribute target did; an int, float, str or tuple item still gets a new value, and `t[0] += [7]` on a tuple changes the list and then raises TypeError as CPython does. The typed path refuses the shape ("container fields are snapshots"). The C of every `d[k] += x` changes (the in-place helper falls through to the plain one). `test_ail_gcroots.AugItemDifferential` (randomized against CPython, general and default builds). |

| C91 | loud, **fixed**; silent, **fixed** | general path: `Counter("ab") + Counter("b")` where the operands are expressions or arguments (also `-`, `&`, `|`, unary `+` / `-`, `c += d`, `c -= d`, `c &= d`, `c |= d`); `print(c)` of a Counter; `Counter({"a": 3})` | `TypeError: unsupported operand type(s) for +: 'dict' and 'dict'`; `print(c)` showed `{'a': 1}` (a plain dict, insertion order); `Counter(mapping)` counted the keys (`Counter({"a": 3})` gave `{'a': 1}`); `+x` returned x unchanged | `Counter({'b': 2, 'a': 1})` | Fixed (branch deqctr, placeholder number): a Counter is a dict whose header carries the flag, so the operators work on any operand: the entries of the left operand first, then the keys only the right one has (`+ - |`), only positive counts kept, `-` turns the negative counts of the right operand positive, `&` keeps the smaller count, `|` the larger, unary `+` keeps the positive counts and unary `-` the negative ones with the sign flipped; the in-place forms store the result into the same object. A Counter with a dict (or anything else) raises `unsupported operand type(s) for +: 'Counter' and 'dict'`. `repr` is `Counter({...})` in `most_common` order (equal counts in insertion order) and `Counter()` when empty; `Counter(mapping)` takes the counts; `.copy()` and `copy.copy` stay Counters, `dict(c)` does not. `+x` on a value of unknown kind goes through `ail_op_pos` (`+"s"` raises TypeError). The refusal of C106 / C107 is a flow-insensitive scan (`dictviews.stored`), not a proof: a kept view is refused when it sits in a field, item or display that is stored, is returned / stored / passed to a function of the program, or can be read after a statement that changes a dict (an item store or delete, `|=`, a call of `pop` / `update` / `clear` / `setdefault` ..., a call of a function or method of the program that has one) or in one loop with such a statement. This over-refuses (a view rebound in every iteration of a loop that also stores into the dict; a view kept in a field and only read). Not seen: a view handed to a built-in that calls back, a changed dict reached through an unknown callback or a `__setitem__` of a user class, a `defaultdict` read that inserts. 0 of the 510 corpus programs are refused newly. Workaround: call `d.keys()` where it is used, or `list(d.keys())`. |

Compile time, both paths, naming C114: an `is` / `is not` where an operand is statically an int or a number that may be one (`len(x)`, `int(s)`, `n + 1` with a number, a local only bound to such values, a parameter annotated `int`, a `range` loop variable), unless the other side is None, True, False, a str or bytes constant. Corpus `~/corpus/gdev` (510 programs): 15 programs have an `is` that is not against None / True / False; none has a number operand (enums, classes, lists, `type(x) is`), so 0 programs change. | C115 | refused (default build and general path) | `w = 2.0; print(w is 2.0)` and `xs = [1.5, 2.5, 1.5]; xs[0] is xs[2]` (equal constants of one code object) | CPython merges equal constants of a code object into one object, AIL boxes each literal separately | `True` | **Refused** (branch silent2, 2026-10-21, `numidentity.py`) for the forms this scan sees, naming C115: an `is` / `is not` with a float literal or an int literal outside -5..256 (`t is 2.5`, `a is -6`, `x is 257`), with a name only ever bound to constants of one scope (`a = 2.0; b = 2.0; a is b`), or with an item `xs[i]` (constant index) of a list / tuple written with number literals that is never stored into or mutated by a method (`xs = [1.5, 2.5, 1.5]; xs[0] is xs[2]`). Matching CPython would mean one box per distinct literal for the whole module (3.12 shares the constant cache of one compilation, so `def f(): return 1.5` and `def g(): return 1.5` give one object), not done. **Not refused** (narrowed after review): an int literal of -5..256 (`x is 0`, `x is not 256`, `n is -5`) against an int-typed or unknown operand. CPython caches those ints, so the answer is deterministic: `==` when the other operand is an int, False when it is a bool, a float or anything else (`True is 1`, `0 is False` are False). The typed lowering and the general path (`ail_is`: tags differ, so False; two ints compare by value) do the same, and the `ail_is` C114 stop cannot fire against such a literal (it needs two equal ints outside the range). Also not refused: an object compared with an expression built from itself (`(x or "o") is not x`, `h[1] is h[1]`), which asks about one object, not two merged constants (this is the form `test_ail_deqctr` uses with names bound to `0.0`, `2.5`, `-3`; an equal int outside -5..256 there still meets the C114 run-time stop). Residual: see C115-silent2-1. Was: found (branch unified5) while testing C102 (`g(w, 2.0)`); `AIL_NO_MIX=1` too.
