A fix without the name scan needs a NaN-aware box (a NaN keeps its box when stored). |
| C119 | silent / loud, **fixed** | general path: `nan = float("nan"); xs = [nan]; xs.count(nan); xs.index(nan)` | `1`, `0` | `0`, `ValueError: nan is not in list` | **Fixed** (branch silent2, 2026-10-21, `test_ail_silent2` NanIdentity). General path (boxed floats, which have an identity): `list.count`, `index`, `remove`, tuple `count` / `index`, deque `count` / `index` / `remove` use `ail_member_eq` (identity, then `==`), like `in`, dict lookup and list `==`; two `float('nan')` objects stay unequal; `-0.0` / `0.0` / inf unchanged. Typed path (floats are unboxed doubles, a NaN has no identity there): **kept, with a loud run-time stop for a NaN argument** (changed after review: the first version refused these calls at compile time, which sent common code such as `xs.index(max(xs))` to the general path, about 20x slower, for a rare NaN). A `count` / `index` / `remove` of a float in a list of floats, a `count` on a float list field and `x in d.values()` of a dict of floats stay on the typed path. For an argument that is not NaN the typed `==` answer is exactly CPython's (identity cannot change a non-NaN `==`), including a NaN element, `-0.0` / `0.0` and inf. If the searched value is NaN the program stops with `NaN searched with list.index` (or `list.count`, `list.remove`, `x in d.values()`) `... (DIFFERENCES C119)`, the same style as the C114 stop; it is not catchable and not silent. Cost: one `x != x` test per call, outside the element loop (the loop C is unchanged); about 1 instruction per call on a 1000-element search, 3 on an 8-element one. Not touched (nothing to compare by identity): int / str lists, plain `for` and `sum` of floats. Typed `==` / `in` of float lists and tuples was already refused or stopped (`a tuple comparison reached a nan`). Deques (added after a second review: the first version fixed `deque([...])` of mixed or untyped items but not a deque of floats that the compiler types): a deque of floats built in a function is a typed `_AilDeque_float` (collib.py, a ring buffer of unboxed doubles). Its `count`, `index`, `remove` and `x in q` had a plain `==` on both paths, so `q.count(nan)` was `0` and `nan in q` `False` where CPython answers `1` and `True`. General path (forced): the helper's `==` is `ail_member_eq` (identity, then `==`), so `deque([n, 3.0])` gives `1 1 True` and `remove(n)` works, as CPython. Default build: the typed `_AilDeque_float` path is **kept** for these four operations (no compile-time refusal, no fallback to the general path), exactly as for the typed float list: one `x != x` test on the searched argument per call, outside the element loop, and a NaN argument stops by name (`NaN searched with deque.count`, `deque.index`, `deque.remove` or `x in deque`, `... (DIFFERENCES C119)`, not catchable, not silent); any other argument answers as CPython. Also fixed: the typed helper's `deque.remove` error text now is CPython 3.12's `<repr> is not in deque`. A deque that reaches the search through an attribute, an argument, a list item or a dict value, or holds mixed or untyped items, is the runtime deque (a wrapper list) and already used `ail_member_eq` on both paths. Probed and already CPython on both paths, no change: `Counter` / `dict` / `defaultdict` keys and `set` / `frozenset` membership, `add`, `discard` with a NaN key (`c[n]` finds the same NaN object, a second `float('nan')` is a new key, `len` agrees; `test_ail_silent2` NanKeys). The v1 runtime (`AIL_GENERAL=v1`, the way back) stops with a named message when a NaN argument meets a NaN element in `count` / `index`. Found (branch udefault) while reproducing C117; the same with and without the flag. Was: open.

| C140 | silent / loud, **fixed** | default build (AIL_UNIFIED on): `big = 1e308 * 10; m = big - big; xs = [m, 1.0]; xs.count(m); xs.index(m); xs.remove(m); m in xs; [m] == [m]`, and the same through a parameter, a list item, an attribute, a dict value, a dataclass field, a dict key | `1 0 1`, `2 1`, `True`... (the one object `m`) | `ValueError: nan is not in list` (exit 1); `AIL_UNIFIED=0` was right | **Fixed** (branch nanuni, 2026-10-21, `test_ail_nan_arith`, `test_ail_silent2` NanIdentity). This is the C118 risk, now reproduced: the C117 guard `nan_capable` looks for the *names* nan / inf / isnan in the user's source, and this program spells none (the NaN is `inf - inf` made by arithmetic on an overflow; it could equally be `float(s)` or parsed data). The guard scanned the right tree (the user's source, `mix_tree`) and ran (`AIL_UNIFIED_FORCE` only forces the general path); it cannot see the value. First fix (`unified.float_search_risk`; the fallback when the narrow proof below does not apply): when a program has an unboxed float anywhere and searches a container (`count` / `index` / `remove` / `get` / `pop` / `setdefault` / `discard`, `in`, `not in`, `==` / `!=` between non-scalars, a dict lookup by a float / tuple / instance) with a value that is not provably float-free, no float is unboxed in the whole program (they keep the general path's one box per value, so identity is as in CPython). Float-free: constants, f-strings, int / bool values, `str`-method results, and locals that only hold those and are only read (never appended to, stored into, passed on, aliased or captured). The program's own code only: preludes, libraries and the generated dataclass `__eq__` are not scanned (reached only through a comparison written in the program). `AIL_UNIFIED_STATS` records `floats_kept_boxed` (the first site). Second fix (review, 2026-10-21): `unified.float_escape_demotes` keeps only the float locals that may be boxed twice or whose box may be shared (a class of locals joined by copies; one boxing point once per call of a value made in the function is allowed; a parameter, a read from a boxed object or a boxing point in a loop is not) boxed, and the lists and tuples that are searched or compared keep their float elements boxed (`unified_lists.solve(floatguard)`); a loop of plain float arithmetic stays unboxed. When a float is read out of a typed list and boxed again the proof does not apply and every float stays boxed as before. `AIL_UNIFIED_STATS`: `float_search_site`, `float_locals_kept_boxed`. |

| C159 | silent, **fixed** | `deque(..., maxlen=k) * n` and `d *= n` where the deque keeps a maxlen, including `k = 0`, an empty deque, `n = 0` and a negative `n` (both builds) | the FIRST `k` items of the repeated sequence: `deque([1, 2, 3], maxlen=4) * 2` was `deque([1, 2, 3, 1], maxlen=4)`, and `d *= 2` changed the aliased deque the same wrong way | `deque([3, 1, 2, 3], maxlen=4)` (CPython keeps the LAST `k`); `n <= 0` and `maxlen=0` give an empty deque with the same maxlen | **Fixed** (branch left1oc, 2026-10-22, `test_ail_left1.LeftOneTests.test_deque_maxlen_mul`): `ail_deque_repeat` (cruntime.py) copies the final `min(n * len(data), maxlen)` items of the repeated sequence. Its length is a multiple of the data length, so the copy starts at `(len - count % len) % len`; an unbounded deque still repeats the whole data. `ail_op_imul` stores that data into the same wrapper, so an alias sees the new contents and `maxlen` is unchanged. Probed with `maxlen` 0, 1, 4 and 10, an empty data list, an unbounded deque, `n` 0, 1, 2, 3, 1000 and -1 / -2, both `q * n` and `n * q` (original unchanged), and `d *= n` through an alias, both builds. |

Every member is validated before an invalid window answers `False`; a matching early member on a valid window still short-circuits. |

The general path evaluates each bound once and converts int / bool / `__index__`; list, tuple and deque share the check, str's None-as-default is unchanged. The typed lowering keeps visible int/bool bounds, raises for visibly non-index ones and leaves the rest to the general path; receiver class resolution is cycle-safe and rebinding forms count as unknown. |

| C223 | nested defs / default arguments, **fixed; `test_ail_fuzz94.py`** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | A mutable literal default of a nested `def` directly in its enclosing body was rebuilt at each call site, so `def f(x, acc=[]); f(1); f(2)` printed `[1] [2]` where CPython shares the list built when the `def` ran and prints `[1] [1, 2]`. | C228 | enumerate / int64 counter, **fixed / refused by name; `test_ail_pre186.py`** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | An `enumerate` start close to `INT64_MAX`: values past `INT64_MAX` cannot be represented (AIL ints are 64-bit), so the counter now stops with `OverflowError: enumerate() index integer overflow` instead of silently wrapping to `INT64_MIN`; CPython prints the arbitrary-precision value. | C230 | bytes/bytearray padding fillchar, **fixed** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | `ljust`, `rjust` and `center` converted the fillchar with the generic bytes helper before checking its type, so a str, int, bool, list or user-class fillchar raised `a bytes-like object is required, not '<type>'` instead of CPython's `<method>() argument 2 must be a byte string of length 1, not <type>`. | C231 | integers / unary constants, **fixed** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | A statically known unary integer expression outside int64 (`-MIN` on a module constant, or `-(-9223372036854775808)`) used to compile on the default build and trap at run time with `integer overflow`; CPython prints a bignum. | C232 | contextlib `@contextmanager` / `StopIteration`, **fixed** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | A `StopIteration` raised in a `with` body is sent back into the generator by `__exit__`; CPython's PEP 479 wraps it in a `RuntimeError` whose `__cause__` is that object, and `contextlib` then re-raises the original `StopIteration`. AIL converted it to `RuntimeError: generator raised StopIteration` on both builds. The runtime now keeps that cause identity (hidden from ordinary attribute reads, which still refuse chaining) and `_AilGenCM.__exit__` follows CPython's `contextlib` check. A generator that catches the sent value and raises a different `StopIteration` still yields `RuntimeError`. Covered by `test_ail_pre181.py`. |
| C233 | `with` / `__exit__` traceback argument, **fixed; `test_ail_pre197.py`** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | `__exit__` receives `tb=None` where CPython passes the active traceback object. | C234 | `format()` of None / an instance without `__format__`, **fixed; `test_ail_pre102.py`** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | The general runtime parsed the spec before checking the value, so `format(None, ' +8')` and `format(Box(1), ' +8')` raised `ValueError: Invalid format specifier` where CPython raises `TypeError: unsupported format string passed to NoneType.__format__` / `Box.__format__`. | C239 | module-scope annotations, **fixed** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | Both builds dropped a module-level `target: annotation` without a value, so `touch(b).x: ann()` never called `ann` and `xs[touch(i)]: ann()` never evaluated its target; annotated assignments with a value also skipped the annotation. | C241 | generator expressions, **open** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | The pre116 refusal leaves a saved generator eager only when its body calls the builtin `next` (not rebound or deleted) or a plain class (no bases, decorators, metaclass, `__new__`, `__slots__` or attribute hooks) whose `__init__` only binds its parameters or constants to `self`. A rebound/deleted class, a shadowed `next`, an inherited or rebound constructor and any other operand, call or free name are refused by name. If the allowed `next`/constructor raises at `next()` time it is still raised when the generator is made; the other raisable bodies remain refused. |
| C242 | generator expressions, **refused** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | A generator expression saved as a value (assignment, argument, list item, attribute, dict value or return) was materialised eagerly on the general path. The general path now refuses a saved generator whose lazy body can raise, by the typed path's own name "a generator expression used as a value"; immediate consumers and tuple unpacking keep the eager lowering. `test_ail_pre116.py`. |
| C243 | mixed-mode exception propagation, **fixed** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | A typed clone of a helper that could raise was selected even when the helper was called from a lambda or through a function passed as a value. | C244 | integer `% 0`, **fixed** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | The general path and the default build's fallback named `ZeroDivisionError: integer division or modulo by zero` for `%`, which CPython uses only for `//` and `divmod`; `%` now says `integer modulo by zero` on both builds. | C245 | nested def defaults, **fixed; `test_ail_pre117.py`** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | A nested `def` with a literal list/dict/set default (or a tuple holding one) rebuilt it at every call, so `def f(x, acc=[]); f(1); f(2)` printed `[1] [2]` where CPython shares the definition-time object and prints `[1] [1, 2]`. The nested default is now evaluated once at the `def` into a hidden local (see C267), so it is shared between calls. |
| C246 | class attribute read through a class value, **fixed; `test_ail_pre170.py`** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | A class body with a duplicate assignment (`class C: x = 1; x = 2`) read through an alias, an argument, a list/dict item or `getattr()` returned the first value, or raised `AttributeError` for an aliased class, where CPython keeps the last definition. Class-body entries are registered in source order and the runtime lookup scans each class backwards, so the last definition wins; a module-level rebinding of the class name is seen through the class, the array-entry shape initialises the class bodies, and a method/property read through a statically known class value is refused by name. |
| C247 | integer constant expressions, **fixed** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | An all-int constant expression whose exact result fits int64 but whose intermediate does not (for example `(1 << 63) - 1` or `2**65 - 2**65`) was emitted as runtime checked operations and stopped with `integer overflow`. The typed IR, legacy typed emitter and both general emitters now fold the whole expression with Python ints and emit the in-range result. |
| C248 | collections.Counter constructor/update, **fixed; `test_ail_pre120.py`** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | CPython 3.12's `Counter.update(mapping)` copies the mapping with `dict.update` when the counter is empty and only adds counts when it is not, so `Counter({"a": "x"})` keeps `"x"`; the general path added `0 + "x"` and raised `TypeError` (`Counter().update({"a": "x"})` did the same). Empty mapping updates now copy values; non-empty updates still add in CPython's operand order. | C249 | `int(s, base)` invalid literal message, **fixed; `test_ail_pre180.py`** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | An invalid literal containing a non-ASCII printable character kept only the low byte of each code point (`int("\u2469", 10)` printed `...: 'i'` where CPython prints `'\u2469'`), and the message did not apply CPython's 200-character repr cut. The general path now composes the message from the repr string, and both paths cut the repr after 200 code points like CPython's `%.200R`. |
| C250 | itertools / permutations, **`itertools.permutations(xs, None)` / `r=None` returned `[()]` on the general path instead of the full-length permutations. The typed path already handled the literal; it refuses a None-typed variable by name.** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | fixed |
| C251 | round, **The default build stopped on one-argument `round()` of nan/inf through the runtime trap (`round() of nan`, `round() result does not fit...`), so a `try` could not catch it and an uncaught program printed the trap instead of CPython's exception. Both builds now raise ValueError / OverflowError with CPython's text; finite values, including the int64-boundary trap, are unchanged.** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | fixed |
| C252 | class constants / module names, **fixed / refused; `test_ail_pre227.py`** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | A class-level assignment that built a constant from a module name (`N = 2**62; class C: X = N * 4`) evaluated checked int64 arithmetic in the class body, so it stopped with `integer overflow` where CPython keeps an unbounded int; `N = 2**61; class C: X = N * 2 - 1` trapped although the final 2**63-1 fits. `fold_constants` now resolves a module name bound once before the class into class-level constant expressions and folds them exactly with wider steps allowed: an in-range value is emitted as a literal, and a value that cannot fit int64 is refused by name (`int64 literal out of range`, or `int64 constant expression out of range` when the current module/class constant check sees the folded literal) on both builds. Values that only reach the class body at run time (a list item, an attribute, a dict value, an argument) keep the fixed-width runtime trap, and a class definition inside a function is refused by name. |
| C253 | integer constant folding / large special exponents, **fixed / refused by name; `test_ail_pre236.py`** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | The generic folders evaluated both operands before the zero/base shortcuts, so `0 * (1 << 2000000)`, `0 ** (1 << 2000000)`, `1 ** (1 << 2000000)` and `(-1) ** (1 << 2000000)` (also through a module/class/default, an argument, a list item, an attribute and a dict value) were refused although every result fits int64. An absorbing zero and the power bases 0/1/-1 are now decided before the size guard, as `pyfront.int_constant` already did, and a huge exponent of such a power is retried at the 2**24-bit special limit for its sign and parity; an absorbing zero also folds with an operand above that limit (`0 * (1 << 16777216)`). A non-special intermediate beyond 2**20 bits and a special exponent beyond 2**24 bits remain refused by name. The two stale `test_ail_pre121` expectations now also accept pre177's `int64 constant out of range` wording for the same named refusal. |
| C254 | integer constants / huge diagnostics, **fixed; `test_ail_pre238.py`** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | A constant `**` whose exact value exceeds CPython's 4300-digit int-to-str limit (for example a 3000-digit base to the 64th) made the compiler's own `str(value)` fail while it formatted `int64 constant out of range`, so both builds stopped with `Exceeds the limit (4300 digits) ...` (exit 2) instead of a named refusal. The diagnostic now prints `too large` for such values; the index, literal and unary messages use the same guard. The pre159 reviewer's `print(10 ** 4000 * 10 ** 300)` is already refused by name by the front end on this tree. |
| C255 | bool / constant shifts, **fixed / refused by name; `test_ail_pre241.py`** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | A bool is an int subclass in CPython, but the constant folders accepted only `type(x) is int`, so `True << 64`, `True << 4096`, `(True << 100000) >> 100000`, `if False: print(True << 64)` and `(True << 64) >> 64` compiled with fixed-width arithmetic and stopped with `integer overflow`, where CPython prints the bignum or 1. Bool constants now fold exactly like int constants: an in-range result (including a smaller result reached through a larger intermediate) is emitted as a literal, and an out-of-int64 result is refused by name with the existing `integer constant expression ... signed 64-bit range` wording. A bool result of `&`, `|`, `^` is still left to the runtime so `True & False` prints `False`; negative counts still raise `ValueError`. |
| C256 | module-level function rebinding, **fixed / partly refused by name; `test_ail_pre242.py`** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | A top-level `def f` was always called through its compiled function, so a later module-scope `f = lambda: ...` (or an assignment in a module-level `if`/loop) was ignored and `f()` still printed the old result. The general path now binds the function value into the module slot at its `def` and routes calls through that slot whenever the name is assigned at module scope, for zero/one/two parameters, another function, a list item, an attribute, a dict value and a function argument; calls before and after the rebind and rebinding to a non-callable (TypeError) match CPython on both builds. Rebinding a function whose value cannot be built (`def f(a, b=3)`; a keyword call through the rebound value) is refused by name. A `global f` write inside a function is a separate storage path and is not covered. |
| C257 | v1 general path / C names, **fixed; `test_ail_pre246.py`** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | The v1 emitter (`AIL_GENERAL=v1`, used only when the typed path falls back) pulled in the DIE block and then redefined its `ail_exc_top`, `ail_exc_stack`, `ail_die` and `ail_alloc`, so every v1 program failed to compile (`redefinition of 'ail_exc_top'`). The v1 frame stack and current-exception slot now use `ail_v1_*` names and the v1 emitter reads the renamed slot; the duplicate `ail_die`/`ail_alloc` definitions are gone, so the DIE versions are used. v1 C now builds and runs, including `try`/`except` with an exception variable, nested `try`/`finally`/re-raise and uncaught exceptions. |
| C258 | int64 / constant index INT64_MIN, **fixed; `test_ail_pre246.py`** | see disposition | see disposition | see disposition | The pre177 constant-index check folded unary minus with the per-step int64 bound, so `d[-(1 << 63)]` was refused as `int64 index out of range: 9223372036854775808` although INT64_MIN is representable and CPython uses it as an ordinary key. Unary `-`/`~` now fold their operand exactly and range-check only the result, so INT64_MIN and `~INT64_MIN` (INT64_MAX) work as list/tuple/str/bytes/dict indexes, including through a parameter, a list item, an attribute, a dict value and an alias. Values whose result still leaves int64 (for example `1 << 63`, `-(1 << 63) - 1`) keep the by-name refusal. |

In-range constants, invalid literals/bases and dynamic strings keep their run-time parse. |

These rows were in the public `docs/spec/DIFFERENCES.md` before the private ledger above was published.
They are kept verbatim so no row is lost; several are the same changes that the private ledger numbers as C4xx.


This CI export does not carry the full development ledger.  The row closed by this job is kept here
so the change is recorded with the code it describes.

| id | status | difference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C?-job-rbfuzz59-10271222-1 | refused by name; `test_ail_pre19.py` | `del` with a constant index or slice bound whose exact final value is beyond int64 (`del a[2**63]`, `del a[-2**63 - 1]`, `del a[2**100:2**101]`, also through a nested item) is now refused at compile time on both builds instead of trapping `integer overflow`; the whole expression is folded with Python integers, so an out-of-range intermediate whose final value fits (`del a[2**63 * 0]`) still builds, as do `del a[2**63 - 1]`, `-2**63` and the same expression outside a `del`. |
| C?-job-rbpre172-10251601-1 | fixed | A statically known unary integer expression outside int64 (`-MIN` on a module constant, or `-(-9223372036854775808)`) used to compile on the default build and trap at run time with `integer overflow`; CPython prints a bignum. Both builds now refuse it by name (`int64 constant expression out of range: ...`); variables that only reach the operation at run time keep the fixed-width trap. Covered by `test_ail_pre172.py`. |
| C169 | closed | `list.index` / `tuple.index` read a `None`, float or str bound as index 0 instead of raising CPython's `TypeError: slice indices must be integers or have an __index__ method`.  Both bounds are now converted at run time (int / bool / `__index__`); the typed lowering handles visible int/bool and non-index bounds and leaves the rest to that path, and a bad `__index__` return is CPython's `__index__ returned non-int (type ...)` with the complete type name.  Covered by `test_ail_c169.py`. |

| C?-job-fuzz70-10231500-1 | assert | A typed-only (`--strict` / `AIL_NO_GENERAL=1`) build refuses `assert cond, tuple_message` by name. The default build and the general path keep the message object in `AssertionError.args`, as CPython does. |
| id | difference |
| --- | --- |
| C?-job-fuzz82-10231553-1 | A function that deletes an item of a module-level numeric list (`del xs[i]`) is refused by name on the typed path. Before, deleting the last item printed the unchanged list (the write through the `const` table was dropped) and deleting an earlier item crashed; the default build falls back to the general path and matches CPython. |
| id | status | area | summary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C?-job-fuzz61-10231419-1 | fixed | string encoding | A run-time encoding name used to accept only a few spellings of utf-8, ascii and latin-1, so aliases reached through a variable, argument, list item, attribute or dict value raised `LookupError`. Every alias from `encodings.aliases` for those three codecs is now accepted on both builds. |
| C?-job-fuzz61-10231419-2 | open | string encoding | A run-time name of a codec outside utf-8/ascii/latin-1 (for example `cp1252`, `utf-8-sig`, `utf-16`) raises `LookupError` where CPython encodes or decodes. The same name written as a literal is refused at compile time by name. |
| C?-job-fuzz61-10231419-3 | open | string encoding | `b"".decode(name)` looks the codec up and raises `LookupError` for an unknown name; CPython returns `b""` without looking it up. `"".encode(name)` does validate the name in both. |
| C?-job-fuzz61-10231419-4 | open | exceptions | `UnicodeEncodeError` / `UnicodeDecodeError` values do not expose `.encoding`, `.object`, `.start`, `.end` or `.reason`. |
